This program is the **core of Phase 2: Real-Time Network Data Collection**. I’ll explain it in the exact order in which the program works.\\n\\n# 1. Importing the libraries\\n\\n```python\\nimport psutil\\nimport time\\nimport platform\\nimport subprocess\\nimport re\\n```\\n\\nThese libraries provide different functions:\\n\\n| Library | Purpose |\\n|---|---|\\n| `psutil` | Gets network information from the operating system |\\n| `time` | Adds delays and gets the current time |\\n| `platform` | Identifies whether the system is Windows/Linux/etc. |\\n| `subprocess` | Runs the system `ping` command |\\n| `re` | Finds the latency value from the ping output |\\n\\n### In simple words\\n\\nWe are using Python to communicate with the operating system and collect network information.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 2. Finding the active network interface\\n\\n```python\\ndef get_active_interface():\\n\\n    interfaces = psutil.net_if_stats()\\n\\n    for name, stats in interfaces.items():\\n\\n        if stats.isup and name.lower() not in [\\\"lo\\\", \\\"loopback\\\"]:\\n            return name\\n\\n    return None\\n```\\n\\nThis function finds the network interface that is currently active.\\n\\n### Step 1\\n\\n```python\\ninterfaces = psutil.net_if_stats()\\n```\\n\\n`psutil` checks all network interfaces available on the computer.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nWi-Fi\\nEthernet\\nBluetooth\\nLoopback\\nVPN\\n```\\n\\nIt provides information such as:\\n\\n```text\\nisup\\nspeed\\nduplex\\nmtu\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 2\\n\\n```python\\nfor name, stats in interfaces.items():\\n```\\n\\nThe program goes through each network interface one by one.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nWi-Fi\\nEthernet\\nLoopback\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 3\\n\\n```python\\nif stats.isup and name.lower() not in [\\\"lo\\\", \\\"loopback\\\"]:\\n```\\n\\nTwo conditions are checked:\\n\\n**Condition 1:**\\n\\n```python\\nstats.isup\\n```\\n\\nMeans:\\n\\n\u003e Is this network interface currently active?\\n\\n**Condition 2:**\\n\\n```python\\nname.lower() not in [\\\"lo\\\", \\\"loopback\\\"]\\n```\\n\\nThis avoids selecting the loopback interface.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 4\\n\\n```python\\nreturn name\\n```\\n\\nIf an active interface is found, its name is returned.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nWi-Fi\\n```\\n\\nSo:\\n\\n```python\\ninterface = get_active_interface()\\n```\\n\\nmight give:\\n\\n```text\\nWi-Fi\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 3. Collecting raw network data\\n\\n```python\\ndef get_network_stats():\\n\\n    stats = psutil.net_io_counters()\\n\\n    return {\\n        \\\"bytes_sent\\\": stats.bytes_sent,\\n        \\\"bytes_received\\\": stats.bytes_recv,\\n        \\\"packets_sent\\\": stats.packets_sent,\\n        \\\"packets_received\\\": stats.packets_recv\\n    }\\n```\\n\\nThis function collects the basic network counters from the operating system.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Step 1\\n\\n```python\\nstats = psutil.net_io_counters()\\n```\\n\\n`psutil` obtains network I/O statistics.\\n\\nIt can provide:\\n\\n```text\\nbytes sent\\nbytes received\\npackets sent\\npackets received\\nerrors\\ndropped packets\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Step 2\\n\\nThe program creates a Python dictionary:\\n\\n```python\\n{\\n    \\\"bytes_sent\\\": stats.bytes_sent,\\n    \\\"bytes_received\\\": stats.bytes_recv,\\n    \\\"packets_sent\\\": stats.packets_sent,\\n    \\\"packets_received\\\": stats.packets_recv\\n}\\n```\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nbytes_sent = 125000000\\nbytes_received = 850000000\\npackets_sent = 150000\\npackets_received = 210000\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 4. Calculating upload and download speed\\n\\n```python\\ndef calculate_speed(previous, current, elapsed):\\n```\\n\\nThis function calculates how much data was transferred **during the measurement interval**.\\n\\nThe important idea is:\\n\\n```text\\nCurrent value - Previous value\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Upload calculation\\n\\n```python\\nupload_bps = (\\n    current[\\\"bytes_sent\\\"] -\\n    previous[\\\"bytes_sent\\\"]\\n) / elapsed\\n```\\n\\nSuppose:\\n\\n```text\\nPrevious bytes sent = 100 MB\\nCurrent bytes sent = 102 MB\\nTime = 1 second\\n```\\n\\nThen:\\n\\n```text\\n102 - 100 = 2 MB\\n```\\n\\nSo:\\n\\n```text\\nUpload = 2 MB/sec\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Download calculation\\n\\n```python\\ndownload_bps = (\\n    current[\\\"bytes_received\\\"] -\\n    previous[\\\"bytes_received\\\"]\\n) / elapsed\\n```\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nPrevious = 500 MB\\nCurrent = 510 MB\\n```\\n\\nTherefore:\\n\\n```text\\n510 - 500 = 10 MB\\n```\\n\\nSo:\\n\\n```text\\nDownload = 10 MB/sec\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 5. Converting bytes/sec to Mbps\\n\\n```python\\nupload_mbps = (upload_bps * 8) / 1_000_000\\n```\\n\\nWhy multiply by `8`?\\n\\nBecause:\\n\\n```text\\n1 byte = 8 bits\\n```\\n\\nNetwork speeds are normally represented in **bits per second**, not bytes per second.\\n\\nThen:\\n\\n```text\\n1,000,000 bits = 1 Mbps\\n```\\n\\nSo:\\n\\n```text\\nbytes/sec\\n     ↓\\n× 8\\n     ↓\\nbits/sec\\n     ↓\\n÷ 1,000,000\\n     ↓\\nMbps\\n```\\n\\nThe same calculation is done for download speed.\\n\\nFinally:\\n\\n```python\\nreturn upload_mbps, download_mbps\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 6. Measuring latency and packet loss\\n\\n```python\\ndef ping_host(host=\\\"8.8.8.8\\\"):\\n```\\n\\nThis function checks whether the computer can communicate with Google's public DNS server:\\n\\n```text\\n8.8.8.8\\n```\\n\\nIt measures:\\n\\n- latency\\n- basic packet loss\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 7. Detecting the operating system\\n\\n```python\\nsystem = platform.system()\\n```\\n\\nThis identifies the operating system.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nWindows\\nLinux\\nDarwin\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 8. Creating the ping command\\n\\nFor Windows:\\n\\n```python\\nif system == \\\"Windows\\\":\\n\\n    command = [\\n        \\\"ping\\\",\\n        \\\"-n\\\", \\\"1\\\",\\n        \\\"-w\\\", \\\"1000\\\",\\n        host\\n    ]\\n```\\n\\nThis means approximately:\\n\\n```text\\nping 8.8.8.8\\n```\\n\\nwith:\\n\\n```text\\n-n 1\\n```\\n\\nSend one ping.\\n\\nand:\\n\\n```text\\n-w 1000\\n```\\n\\nWait up to 1000 milliseconds.\\n\\n---\\n\\nFor Linux/macOS:\\n\\n```python\\nelse:\\n\\n    command = [\\n        \\\"ping\\\",\\n        \\\"-c\\\", \\\"1\\\",\\n        \\\"-W\\\", \\\"1\\\",\\n        host\\n    ]\\n```\\n\\nThe syntax is different, so the program handles both systems.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 9. Running the ping command\\n\\n```python\\nresult = subprocess.run(\\n    command,\\n    capture_output=True,\\n    text=True\\n)\\n```\\n\\n`subprocess` allows Python to execute a normal operating-system command.\\n\\nSo Python is effectively running:\\n\\n```text\\nping 8.8.8.8\\n```\\n\\nThe result is stored in:\\n\\n```python\\nresult\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 10. Checking whether ping succeeded\\n\\n```python\\nif result.returncode != 0:\\n\\n    return None, 100.0\\n```\\n\\nIf ping fails:\\n\\n```text\\nLatency = None\\nPacket Loss = 100%\\n```\\n\\nSo the program considers that ping attempt lost.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 11. Extracting latency\\n\\n```python\\nmatch = re.search(\\n    r\\\"time[=\u003c]\\\\s*(\\\\d+(?:\\\\.\\\\d+)?)\\\\s*ms\\\",\\n    result.stdout\\n)\\n```\\n\\nThis is using the `re` library.\\n\\nSuppose ping gives:\\n\\n```text\\nReply from 8.8.8.8: bytes=32 time=22ms\\n```\\n\\nThe regular expression searches for:\\n\\n```text\\n22 ms\\n```\\n\\nand extracts:\\n\\n```text\\n22\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 12. Returning latency and packet loss\\n\\nIf latency was successfully found:\\n\\n```python\\nlatency = float(match.group(1))\\n\\nreturn latency, 0.0\\n```\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nLatency = 22 ms\\nPacket Loss = 0%\\n```\\n\\nIf the latency cannot be found:\\n\\n```python\\nreturn None, 100.0\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 13. Getting network interface speed\\n\\n```python\\ndef get_interface_speed(interface):\\n\\n    stats = psutil.net_if_stats()\\n\\n    if interface in stats:\\n\\n        return stats[interface].speed\\n\\n    return 0\\n```\\n\\nThis function gets the reported speed of the selected network interface.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nWi-Fi → 100 Mbps\\n```\\n\\nor:\\n\\n```text\\nEthernet → 1000 Mbps\\n```\\n\\nThe value is later used for estimating network utilization.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 14. Calculating network utilization\\n\\n```python\\ndef calculate_utilization(\\n        upload_mbps,\\n        download_mbps,\\n        interface_speed):\\n```\\n\\nThe formula is:\\n\\n```text\\nUtilization =\\n(Upload + Download)\\n-------------------- × 100\\n Interface Speed\\n```\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nUpload = 5 Mbps\\nDownload = 45 Mbps\\nInterface speed = 100 Mbps\\n```\\n\\nThen:\\n\\n```text\\nUtilization =\\n(5 + 45) / 100 × 100\\n\\n= 50%\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Code\\n\\n```python\\ntotal_mbps = (\\n    upload_mbps +\\n    download_mbps\\n)\\n```\\n\\nFirst, upload and download are added.\\n\\nThen:\\n\\n```python\\nutilization = (\\n    total_mbps /\\n    interface_speed\\n) * 100\\n```\\n\\nFinally:\\n\\n```python\\nreturn min(utilization, 100)\\n```\\n\\nThis prevents the displayed value from going above 100%.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 15. Starting the main program\\n\\n```python\\ndef main():\\n```\\n\\nThis is where all the individual functions are connected together.\\n\\nThink of `main()` as the **controller** of the entire monitoring system.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 16. Find the active interface\\n\\n```python\\ninterface = get_active_interface()\\n```\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nWi-Fi\\n```\\n\\nThen:\\n\\n```python\\nprint(\\\"Network Interface:\\\", interface)\\n```\\n\\ndisplays:\\n\\n```text\\nNetwork Interface: Wi-Fi\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 17. Get interface speed\\n\\n```python\\nif interface:\\n\\n    interface_speed = get_interface_speed(\\n        interface\\n    )\\n```\\n\\nIf the active interface is:\\n\\n```text\\nWi-Fi\\n```\\n\\nthe program obtains its reported speed.\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nInterface Speed: 100 Mbps\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 18. Take the first network reading\\n\\n```python\\nprevious = get_network_stats()\\n```\\n\\nThis is extremely important.\\n\\nThe program needs an **initial reading** before it can calculate speed.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nPrevious bytes sent = 100 MB\\nPrevious bytes received = 500 MB\\n```\\n\\nThen:\\n\\n```python\\nprevious_time = time.time()\\n```\\n\\nstores the time at which this reading was taken.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 19. Start continuous monitoring\\n\\n```python\\nwhile True:\\n```\\n\\nThis means:\\n\\n\u003e Keep running forever until the user stops the program.\\n\\nSo this becomes a **real-time monitoring loop**.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 20. Wait for one second\\n\\n```python\\ntime.sleep(1)\\n```\\n\\nThe program waits approximately one second.\\n\\nWhy?\\n\\nBecause we need two readings:\\n\\n```text\\nReading 1\\n   ↓\\nwait\\n   ↓\\nReading 2\\n```\\n\\nThen we can calculate how much the network changed.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 21. Take a new network reading\\n\\n```python\\ncurrent = get_network_stats()\\n```\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n### Previous\\n\\n```text\\nBytes sent = 100 MB\\nBytes received = 500 MB\\n```\\n\\n### Current\\n\\n```text\\nBytes sent = 102 MB\\nBytes received = 510 MB\\n```\\n\\nNow we can calculate:\\n\\n```text\\nUpload = 2 MB\\nDownload = 10 MB\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 22. Get the current time\\n\\n```python\\ncurrent_time = time.time()\\n```\\n\\nThen:\\n\\n```python\\nelapsed = current_time - previous_time\\n```\\n\\nThis tells us exactly how much time passed between the two readings.\\n\\nNormally it will be approximately:\\n\\n```text\\n1 second\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 23. Calculate upload/download speed\\n\\n```python\\nupload, download = calculate_speed(\\n    previous,\\n    current,\\n    elapsed\\n)\\n```\\n\\nNow the program calculates:\\n\\n```text\\nUpload Speed\\nDownload Speed\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 24. Measure latency and packet loss\\n\\n```python\\nlatency, packet_loss = ping_host()\\n```\\n\\nThe program sends a ping to:\\n\\n```text\\n8.8.8.8\\n```\\n\\nand gets something like:\\n\\n```text\\nLatency = 21 ms\\nPacket Loss = 0%\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 25. Calculate utilization\\n\\n```python\\nutilization = calculate_utilization(\\n    upload,\\n    download,\\n    interface_speed\\n)\\n```\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nUpload = 5 Mbps\\nDownload = 25 Mbps\\nInterface = 100 Mbps\\n```\\n\\nTherefore:\\n\\n```text\\nUtilization = 30%\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 26. Display the results\\n\\nThe program prints:\\n\\n```python\\nprint(\\\"Time:\\\", time.strftime(\\\"%H:%M:%S\\\"))\\n```\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nTime: 19:35:01\\n```\\n\\nThen:\\n\\n```python\\nprint(\\n    \\\"Upload Speed:\\\",\\n    round(upload, 2),\\n    \\\"Mbps\\\"\\n)\\n```\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nUpload Speed: 5.42 Mbps\\n```\\n\\n`round(upload, 2)` means:\\n\\n\u003e Display only 2 decimal places.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 27. Display download speed\\n\\n```python\\nprint(\\n    \\\"Download Speed:\\\",\\n    round(download, 2),\\n    \\\"Mbps\\\"\\n)\\n```\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nDownload Speed: 25.61 Mbps\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 28. Display packets sent\\n\\n```python\\nprint(\\n    \\\"Packets Sent:\\\",\\n    current[\\\"packets_sent\\\"]\\n)\\n```\\n\\nThis comes directly from `psutil`.\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nPackets Sent: 125430\\n```\\n\\n⚠️ Important: this is a **cumulative counter**, not packets per second.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 29. Display packets received\\n\\n```python\\nprint(\\n    \\\"Packets Received:\\\",\\n    current[\\\"packets_received\\\"]\\n)\\n```\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nPackets Received: 182640\\n```\\n\\nAgain, this is a cumulative operating-system counter.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 30. Display latency\\n\\n```python\\nif latency is not None:\\n```\\n\\nIf ping succeeded:\\n\\n```python\\nprint(\\n    \\\"Latency:\\\",\\n    round(latency, 2),\\n    \\\"ms\\\"\\n)\\n```\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nLatency: 21.00 ms\\n```\\n\\nIf ping failed:\\n\\n```python\\nprint(\\n    \\\"Latency: Request timed out\\\"\\n)\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 31. Display packet loss\\n\\n```python\\nprint(\\n    \\\"Packet Loss:\\\",\\n    round(packet_loss, 2),\\n    \\\"%\\\"\\n)\\n```\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nPacket Loss: 0.0 %\\n```\\n\\nor:\\n\\n```text\\nPacket Loss: 100.0 %\\n```\\n\\nfor a failed individual ping.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 32. Display network utilization\\n\\n```python\\nif utilization is not None:\\n```\\n\\nIf the interface speed is available:\\n\\n```python\\nprint(\\n    \\\"Network Utilization:\\\",\\n    round(utilization, 2),\\n    \\\"%\\\"\\n)\\n```\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nNetwork Utilization: 30.42 %\\n```\\n\\nIf interface speed isn't available:\\n\\n```text\\nNetwork Utilization: Not available\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 33. Prepare for the next measurement\\n\\nThis part is **very important**:\\n\\n```python\\nprevious = current\\n```\\n\\nThe current reading becomes the previous reading for the next cycle.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n### First cycle\\n\\n```text\\nPrevious = 100 MB\\nCurrent = 102 MB\\n```\\n\\nAfter calculation:\\n\\n```text\\nPrevious = 102 MB\\n```\\n\\nThen the next reading might be:\\n\\n```text\\nCurrent = 108 MB\\n```\\n\\nSo:\\n\\n```text\\n108 - 102 = 6 MB\\n```\\n\\nThis is how real-time speed is continuously calculated.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 34. Update the previous time\\n\\n```python\\nprevious_time = current_time\\n```\\n\\nNow the current timestamp becomes the previous timestamp for the next calculation.\\n\\nSo every cycle is basically:\\n\\n```text\\nCollect data\\n     ↓\\nWait ~1 second\\n     ↓\\nCollect data again\\n     ↓\\nCompare old vs new\\n     ↓\\nCalculate speed\\n     ↓\\nPing\\n     ↓\\nCalculate latency/loss\\n     ↓\\nCalculate utilization\\n     ↓\\nDisplay results\\n     ↓\\nRepeat\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 35. Starting the program\\n\\nAt the very bottom:\\n\\n```python\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n\\n    main()\\n```\\n\\nThis means:\\n\\n\u003e If this Python file is directly executed, start the `main()` function.\\n\\nSo when you run:\\n\\n```powershell\\npython src\\\\network_monitor.py\\n```\\n\\nPython eventually executes:\\n\\n```python\\nmain()\\n```\\n\\nand the monitoring begins.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Complete project flow\\n\\nYour entire Phase 2 program can be understood like this:\\n\\n```text\\n              COMPUTER NETWORK\\n                     ↓\\n             Operating System\\n                     ↓\\n                  psutil\\n                     ↓\\n        ┌────────────┴────────────┐\\n        ↓                         ↓\\n Network counters             Interface info\\n        ↓                         ↓\\n Bytes sent/received       Interface speed\\n Packets sent/received           ↓\\n        ↓                         ↓\\n        └──────────┬──────────────┘\\n                   ↓\\n             Python Processing\\n                   ↓\\n        ┌──────────┼──────────┐\\n        ↓          ↓          ↓\\n    Upload      Download    Utilization\\n     Speed        Speed\\n        │          │\\n        └────┬─────┘\\n             ↓\\n          ping()\\n             ↓\\n       ┌─────┴─────┐\\n       ↓           ↓\\n    Latency    Packet Loss\\n       │           │\\n       └─────┬─────┘\\n             ↓\\n       Display Results\\n             ↓\\n        Wait 1 second\\n             ↓\\n           Repeat\\n```\\n\\n## Your 8 project metrics\\n\\nThis code currently collects/calculates:\\n\\n| Metric | How it is obtained |\\n|---|---|\\n| **1. Network Traffic** | Bytes sent/received |\\n| **2. Upload Speed** | Difference in sent bytes over time |\\n| **3. Download Speed** | Difference in received bytes over time |\\n| **4. Packets Sent** | `psutil` packet counter |\\n| **5. Packets Received** | `psutil` packet counter |\\n| **6. Packet Loss** | Ping success/failure |\\n| **7. Latency** | Ping response time |\\n| **8. Network Utilization** | Traffic rate ÷ interface speed |\\n\\n### One important correction before you finalize Phase 2\\n\\nThere is a technical issue in the current code: `get_network_stats()` uses:\\n\\n```python\\npsutil.net_io_counters()\\n```\\n\\nwhich collects **all network interfaces together**, while `interface_speed` belongs to only one interface. For a proper project implementation, we should change this to collect data specifically from the selected interface.\\n\\nAlso, your packet-loss calculation currently uses **only one ping per second**, so it is a basic measurement rather than a robust packet-loss percentage.\\n\\n**So I would fix those two things before moving to Phase 3 (database/storage).**\",{},{\"_216\":1116,\"_223\":1137,\"_54\":1138,\"_227\":1139,\"_229\":230,\"_232\":233,\"_234\":1140,\"_236\":237},\"a2f88be8-9f12-4347-9176-c0d5c3c6988e\",[1103],{\"_327\":340,\"_234\":1143},1790096053.758261,{\"_322\":323,\"_324\":1142},{\"_337\":34,\"_1129\":1141,\"_249\":109,\"_254\":255,\"_257\":1127,\"_259\":260,\"_261\":1128,\"_263\":1128,\"_264\":109,\"_66\":67,\"_266\":1135,\"_267\":10,\"_265\":14},\"79a3246a-c2fd-46b2-b8d4-5a8978ceddab\",[339],{},{\"_216\":1135,\"_223\":1147,\"_54\":1148,\"_56\":1149,\"_227\":1150,\"_229\":230,\"_232\":233,\"_234\":1151,\"_236\":237},\"246b6b43-6f02-4080-b827-add71ddfcc14\",[1116],{\"_327\":357,\"_234\":1156},1790096054.481,1790096361.537512,{\"_322\":323,\"_324\":1154},{\"_350\":1152,\"_257\":1127,\"_352\":-5,\"_261\":1128,\"_263\":1128,\"_1129\":1141,\"_267\":10,\"_265\":14},{\"_353\":1153},[],[1155],\"import psutil\\r\\nimport time\\r\\nimport platform\\r\\nimport subprocess\\r\\nimport re\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\r\\n# 1. GET NETWORK INTERFACE SPEED\\r\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\ndef get_interface_speed(interface):\\r\\n\\r\\n    stats = psutil.net_if_stats()\\r\\n\\r\\n    if interface in stats:\\r\\n\\r\\n        return stats[interface].speed\\r\\n\\r\\n    return 0\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\r\\n# 6. CALCULATE NETWORK UTILIZATION\\r\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\ndef calculate_utilization(\\r\\n        upload_mbps,\\r\\n        download_mbps,\\r\\n        interface_speed):\\r\\n\\r\\n    if interface_speed \u003c= 0:\\r\\n\\r\\n        return None\\r\\n\\r\\n    total_mbps = (\\r\\n        upload_mbps +\\r\\n        download_mbps\\r\\n    )\\r\\n\\r\\n    utilization = (\\r\\n        total_mbps /\\r\\n        interface_speed\\r\\n    ) * 100\\r\\n\\r\\n    return min(utilization, 100)\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\r\\n# 7. MAIN REAL-TIME MONITOR\\r\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\ndef main():\\r\\n\\r\\n    interface = get_active_interface()\\r\\n\\r\\n    print(\\\"======================================\\\")\\r\\n    print(\\\"     REAL-TIME NETWORK MONITOR\\\")\\r\\n    print(\\\"======================================\\\")\\r\\n\\r\\n    print(\\\"Network Interface:\\\", interface)\\r\\n\\r\\n    if interface:\\r\\n\\r\\n        interface_speed = get_interface_speed(\\r\\n            interface\\r\\n        )\\r\\n\\r\\n        print(\\r\\n            \\\"Interface Speed:\\\",\\r\\n            interface_speed,\\r\\n            \\\"Mbps\\\"\\r\\n        )\\r\\n\\r\\n    else:\\r\\n\\r\\n        interface_speed = 0\\r\\n\\r\\n        print(\\\"No active network interface found.\\\")\\r\\n\\r\\n    # First network reading\\r\\n    previous = get_network_stats()\\r\\n\\r\\n    previous_time = time.time()\\r\\n\\r\\n    # Continuous monitoring\\r\\n    while True:\\r\\n\\r\\n        # Wait one second\\r\\n        time.sleep(1)\\r\\n\\r\\n        # Get new network reading\\r\\n        current = get_network_stats()\\r\\n\\r\\n        current_time = time.time()\\r\\n\\r\\n        # Calculate actual time difference\\r\\n        elapsed = current_time - previous_time\\r\\n\\r\\n        # Calculate upload/download speed\\r\\n        upload, download = calculate_speed(\\r\\n            previous,\\r\\n            current,\\r\\n            elapsed\\r\\n        )\\r\\n\\r\\n        # Measure latency and packet loss\\r\\n        latency, packet_loss = ping_host()\\r\\n\\r\\n        # Calculate network utilization\\r\\n        utilization = calculate_utilization(\\r\\n            upload,\\r\\n            download,\\r\\n            interface_speed\\r\\n        )\\r\\n\\r\\n        # ------------------------------------------\\r\\n        # DISPLAY RESULTS\\r\\n        # ------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n        print(\\\"\\\\n--------------------------------------\\\")\\r\\n\\r\\n        print(\\r\\n            \\\"Time:\\\",\\r\\n            time.strftime(\\\"%H:%M:%S\\\")\\r\\n        )\\r\\n\\r\\n        print(\\r\\n            \\\"Upload Speed:\\\",\\r\\n            round(upload, 2),\\r\\n            \\\"Mbps\\\"\\r\\n        )\\r\\n\\r\\n        print(\\r\\n            \\\"Download Speed:\\\",\\r\\n            round(download, 2),\\r\\n            \\\"Mbps\\\"\\r\\n        )\\r\\n\\r\\n        print(\\r\\n            \\\"Packets Sent:\\\",\\r\\n            current[\\\"packets_sent\\\"]\\r\\n        )\\r\\n\\r\\n        print(\\r\\n            \\\"Packets Received:\\\",\\r\\n            current[\\\"packets_received\\\"]\\r\\n        )\\r\\n\\r\\n        if latency is not None:\\r\\n\\r\\n            print(\\r\\n                \\\"Latency:\\\",\\r\\n                round(latency, 2),\\r\\n                \\\"ms\\\"\\r\\n            )\\r\\n\\r\\n        else:\\r\\n\\r\\n            print(\\r\\n                \\\"Latency: Request timed out\\\"\\r\\n            )\\r\\n\\r\\n        print(\\r\\n            \\\"Packet Loss:\\\",\\r\\n            round(packet_loss, 2),\\r\\n            \\\"%\\\"\\r\\n        )\\r\\n\\r\\n        if utilization is not None:\\r\\n\\r\\n            print(\\r\\n                \\\"Network Utilization:\\\",\\r\\n                round(utilization, 2),\\r\\n                \\\"%\\\"\\r\\n            )\\r\\n\\r\\n        else:\\r\\n\\r\\n            print(\\r\\n                \\\"Network Utilization: Not available\\\"\\r\\n            )\\r\\n\\r\\n        # ------------------------------------------\\r\\n        # PREPARE FOR NEXT READING\\r\\n        # ------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n        previous = current\\r\\n\\r\\n        previous_time = current_time\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\r\\n# 8. START PROGRAM\\r\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\r\\n\\r\\n    main()\\nexplain the code step by \"l3pjb4\",\"13e206\",\"nyceac\",\"ddyfz1\",\"kxpkr2\",\"txp17l\",\"ll45o5\",\"7o9b93\",\"o685ql\",\"p3ed0d\",\"q9wb80\",\"swvmih\",\"rhxe1j\",\"ieumxj\",\"afzhx7\",\"1lz3o2\",\"wj50z0\",\"pjrve8\",\"6jjce7\",\"iteylt\",\"c4aody\",\"vst69s\",\"fd072j\",\"h2r7t5\",\"um3dxa\",[321],[1268],\"Yes. Let's slow down and do **Phase 2 properly from the beginning**. I'll tell you **exactly where to create the file, what to paste, what every part does, and how to run it.**\\n\\n# Phase 2 — Real-Time Network Data Collection\\n\\nYour current project should look like this:\\n\\n```text\\nReal-Time-Network-Monitoring/\\n│\\n├── data/\\n├── src/\\n├── dashboard/\\n├── database/\\n├── tests/\\n├── screenshots/\\n├── documentation/\\n└── venv/\\n```\\n\\n## Step 1 — Where to save the code?\\n\\nGo to:\\n\\n```text\\nReal-Time-Network-Monitoring\\n        ↓\\n       src\\n```\\n\\nInside `src`, create this file:\\n\\n```text\\nnetwork_monitor.py\\n```\\n\\nSo the exact location is:\\n\\n```text\\nReal-Time-Network-Monitoring/\\n└── src/\\n    └── network_monitor.py\\n```\\n\\n**Do NOT put this code inside `data`, `dashboard`, or `documentation`.**\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 2 — Open `network_monitor.py`\\n\\nPaste the following complete code into it.\\n\\n```python\\nimport psutil\\nimport time\\nimport platform\\nimport subprocess\\nimport re\\n\\n\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n# 1. FIND ACTIVE NETWORK INTERFACE\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef get_active_interface():\\n\\n    interfaces = psutil.net_if_stats()\\n\\n    for name, stats in interfaces.items():\\n\\n        if stats.isup and name.lower() not in [\\\"lo\\\", \\\"loopback\\\"]:\\n            return name\\n\\n    return None\\n\\n\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n# 2. COLLECT RAW NETWORK DATA\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef get_network_stats():\\n\\n    stats = psutil.net_io_counters()\\n\\n    return {\\n        \\\"bytes_sent\\\": stats.bytes_sent,\\n        \\\"bytes_received\\\": stats.bytes_recv,\\n        \\\"packets_sent\\\": stats.packets_sent,\\n        \\\"packets_received\\\": stats.packets_recv\\n    }\\n\\n\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n# 3. CALCULATE UPLOAD AND DOWNLOAD SPEED\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef calculate_speed(previous, current, elapsed):\\n\\n    upload_bps = (\\n        current[\\\"bytes_sent\\\"] -\\n        previous[\\\"bytes_sent\\\"]\\n    ) / elapsed\\n\\n    download_bps = (\\n        current[\\\"bytes_received\\\"] -\\n        previous[\\\"bytes_received\\\"]\\n    ) / elapsed\\n\\n    upload_mbps = (upload_bps * 8) / 1_000_000\\n\\n    download_mbps = (download_bps * 8) / 1_000_000\\n\\n    return upload_mbps, download_mbps\\n\\n\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n# 4. MEASURE LATENCY AND PACKET LOSS\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef ping_host(host=\\\"8.8.8.8\\\"):\\n\\n    system = platform.system()\\n\\n    if system == \\\"Windows\\\":\\n\\n        command = [\\n            \\\"ping\\\",\\n            \\\"-n\\\", \\\"1\\\",\\n            \\\"-w\\\", \\\"1000\\\",\\n            host\\n        ]\\n\\n    else:\\n\\n        command = [\\n            \\\"ping\\\",\\n            \\\"-c\\\", \\\"1\\\",\\n            \\\"-W\\\", \\\"1\\\",\\n            host\\n        ]\\n\\n    result = subprocess.run(\\n        command,\\n        capture_output=True,\\n        text=True\\n    )\\n\\n    if result.returncode != 0:\\n\\n        return None, 100.0\\n\\n    match = re.search(\\n        r\\\"time[=\u003c]\\\\s*(\\\\d+(?:\\\\.\\\\d+)?)\\\\s*ms\\\",\\n        result.stdout\\n    )\\n\\n    if match:\\n\\n        latency = float(match.group(1))\\n\\n        return latency, 0.0\\n\\n    return None, 100.0\\n\\n\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n# 5. GET NETWORK INTERFACE SPEED\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef get_interface_speed(interface):\\n\\n    stats = psutil.net_if_stats()\\n\\n    if interface in stats:\\n\\n        return stats[interface].speed\\n\\n    return 0\\n\\n\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n# 6. CALCULATE NETWORK UTILIZATION\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef calculate_utilization(\\n        upload_mbps,\\n        download_mbps,\\n        interface_speed):\\n\\n    if interface_speed \u003c= 0:\\n\\n        return None\\n\\n    total_mbps = (\\n        upload_mbps +\\n        download_mbps\\n    )\\n\\n    utilization = (\\n        total_mbps /\\n        interface_speed\\n    ) * 100\\n\\n    return min(utilization, 100)\\n\\n\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n# 7. MAIN REAL-TIME MONITOR\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef main():\\n\\n    interface = get_active_interface()\\n\\n    print(\\\"======================================\\\")\\n    print(\\\"     REAL-TIME NETWORK MONITOR\\\")\\n    print(\\\"======================================\\\")\\n\\n    print(\\\"Network Interface:\\\", interface)\\n\\n    if interface:\\n\\n        interface_speed = get_interface_speed(\\n            interface\\n        )\\n\\n        print(\\n            \\\"Interface Speed:\\\",\\n            interface_speed,\\n            \\\"Mbps\\\"\\n        )\\n\\n    else:\\n\\n        interface_speed = 0\\n\\n        print(\\\"No active network interface found.\\\")\\n\\n    # First network reading\\n    previous = get_network_stats()\\n\\n    previous_time = time.time()\\n\\n    # Continuous monitoring\\n    while True:\\n\\n        # Wait one second\\n        time.sleep(1)\\n\\n        # Get new network reading\\n        current = get_network_stats()\\n\\n        current_time = time.time()\\n\\n        # Calculate actual time difference\\n        elapsed = current_time - previous_time\\n\\n        # Calculate upload/download speed\\n        upload, download = calculate_speed(\\n            previous,\\n            current,\\n            elapsed\\n        )\\n\\n        # Measure latency and packet loss\\n        latency, packet_loss = ping_host()\\n\\n        # Calculate network utilization\\n        utilization = calculate_utilization(\\n            upload,\\n            download,\\n            interface_speed\\n        )\\n\\n        # ------------------------------------------\\n        # DISPLAY RESULTS\\n        # ------------------------------------------\\n\\n        print(\\\"\\\\n--------------------------------------\\\")\\n\\n        print(\\n            \\\"Time:\\\",\\n            time.strftime(\\\"%H:%M:%S\\\")\\n        )\\n\\n        print(\\n            \\\"Upload Speed:\\\",\\n            round(upload, 2),\\n            \\\"Mbps\\\"\\n        )\\n\\n        print(\\n            \\\"Download Speed:\\\",\\n            round(download, 2),\\n            \\\"Mbps\\\"\\n        )\\n\\n        print(\\n            \\\"Packets Sent:\\\",\\n            current[\\\"packets_sent\\\"]\\n        )\\n\\n        print(\\n            \\\"Packets Received:\\\",\\n            current[\\\"packets_received\\\"]\\n        )\\n\\n        if latency is not None:\\n\\n            print(\\n                \\\"Latency:\\\",\\n                round(latency, 2),\\n                \\\"ms\\\"\\n            )\\n\\n        else:\\n\\n            print(\\n                \\\"Latency: Request timed out\\\"\\n            )\\n\\n        print(\\n            \\\"Packet Loss:\\\",\\n            round(packet_loss, 2),\\n            \\\"%\\\"\\n        )\\n\\n        if utilization is not None:\\n\\n            print(\\n                \\\"Network Utilization:\\\",\\n                round(utilization, 2),\\n                \\\"%\\\"\\n            )\\n\\n        else:\\n\\n            print(\\n                \\\"Network Utilization: Not available\\\"\\n            )\\n\\n        # ------------------------------------------\\n        # PREPARE FOR NEXT READING\\n        # ------------------------------------------\\n\\n        previous = current\\n\\n        previous_time = current_time\\n\\n\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n# 8. START PROGRAM\\n# --------------------------------------------------\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n\\n    main()\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 3 — Understand what this code is doing\\n\\nDon't worry about understanding all the code at once.\\n\\nThe program has **8 parts**.\\n\\n```text\\n1. Find network interface\\n        ↓\\n2. Collect raw data\\n        ↓\\n3. Calculate upload/download\\n        ↓\\n4. Measure latency/packet loss\\n        ↓\\n5. Get interface speed\\n        ↓\\n6. Calculate utilization\\n        ↓\\n7. Display everything\\n        ↓\\n8. Repeat continuously\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 4 — Part 1: Find network interface\\n\\n```python\\ndef get_active_interface():\\n```\\n\\nThis function checks your computer's network interfaces.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nWi-Fi\\nEthernet\\nBluetooth\\nLoopback\\n```\\n\\nIt finds an interface that is currently active.\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nNetwork Interface: Wi-Fi\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 5 — Part 2: Collect raw data\\n\\n```python\\nstats = psutil.net_io_counters()\\n```\\n\\nThis is very important.\\n\\n`psutil` asks the operating system:\\n\\n\u003e \\\"How much network data has this computer sent and received?\\\"\\n\\nIt gives us:\\n\\n```text\\nbytes_sent\\nbytes_received\\npackets_sent\\npackets_received\\n```\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nBytes Sent       = 500 MB\\nBytes Received   = 2 GB\\nPackets Sent     = 500,000\\nPackets Received = 800,000\\n```\\n\\nThese are **cumulative values**.\\n\\nThey keep increasing while your computer uses the network.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 6 — Why do we need two readings?\\n\\nSuppose:\\n\\n### First reading\\n\\n```text\\nBytes Received = 100 MB\\n```\\n\\nAfter one second:\\n\\n### Second reading\\n\\n```text\\nBytes Received = 105 MB\\n```\\n\\nTherefore:\\n\\n```text\\n105 - 100\\n= 5 MB\\n```\\n\\nSo your computer received approximately:\\n\\n```text\\n5 MB in 1 second\\n```\\n\\nThat allows us to calculate **download speed**.\\n\\nThe same idea is used for upload speed.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 7 — Upload/download calculation\\n\\nThe code:\\n\\n```python\\nupload_bps = (\\n    current[\\\"bytes_sent\\\"] -\\n    previous[\\\"bytes_sent\\\"]\\n) / elapsed\\n```\\n\\nmeans:\\n\\n```text\\nCurrent bytes sent\\n        -\\nPrevious bytes sent\\n        ↓\\nBytes sent during interval\\n        ↓\\nDivide by time\\n        ↓\\nBytes per second\\n```\\n\\nThen:\\n\\n```python\\nupload_mbps = (upload_bps * 8) / 1_000_000\\n```\\n\\nWhy multiply by 8?\\n\\nBecause:\\n\\n```text\\n1 Byte = 8 bits\\n```\\n\\nNetwork speed is normally represented in:\\n\\n```text\\nMbps\\n```\\n\\nSo we convert bytes/second → bits/second → Mbps.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 8 — Packets sent and received\\n\\nWe get these directly from:\\n\\n```python\\npsutil.net_io_counters()\\n```\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nPackets Sent     = 125,430\\nPackets Received = 182,640\\n```\\n\\nThese values will continuously increase.\\n\\nLater, when we store the data, we'll also calculate **packets per second** if useful for the analytics.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 9 — Latency\\n\\nWe use:\\n\\n```text\\nping → 8.8.8.8\\n```\\n\\nThe computer sends a small network request and waits for the response.\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nRequest\\n   ↓\\n8.8.8.8\\n   ↓\\nResponse\\n```\\n\\nIf the response takes:\\n\\n```text\\n21 ms\\n```\\n\\nthen:\\n\\n```text\\nLatency = 21 ms\\n```\\n\\nLower latency generally means the response is arriving faster.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 10 — Packet loss\\n\\nIf the ping receives a response:\\n\\n```text\\nPacket Loss = 0%\\n```\\n\\nIf the request fails:\\n\\n```text\\nPacket Loss = 100%\\n```\\n\\nWith repeated measurements later, we'll be able to calculate a more meaningful packet-loss percentage over a time window.\\n\\n**Important:** this is why we are not treating `psutil`'s `dropin/dropout` counters as Internet packet loss. They represent interface-level dropped packets, which is a different measurement.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 11 — Network utilization\\n\\nSuppose your network interface reports:\\n\\n```text\\nInterface speed = 100 Mbps\\n```\\n\\nAnd currently:\\n\\n```text\\nUpload = 10 Mbps\\nDownload = 40 Mbps\\n```\\n\\nTotal:\\n\\n```text\\n10 + 40 = 50 Mbps\\n```\\n\\nUtilization:\\n\\n```text\\n50 / 100 × 100\\n= 50%\\n```\\n\\nSo:\\n\\n```text\\nNetwork Utilization = 50%\\n```\\n\\nAgain, this is **interface-link utilization**, not necessarily utilization of your ISP plan.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 12 — Why `while True`?\\n\\nThis is what makes the program **real-time**.\\n\\n```python\\nwhile True:\\n```\\n\\nmeans:\\n\\n\u003e Keep monitoring continuously.\\n\\nThen:\\n\\n```python\\ntime.sleep(1)\\n```\\n\\nwaits approximately one second.\\n\\nSo the program does:\\n\\n```text\\nCollect\\n ↓\\nCalculate\\n ↓\\nDisplay\\n ↓\\nWait 1 second\\n ↓\\nCollect again\\n ↓\\nCalculate\\n ↓\\nDisplay\\n ↓\\n...\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 13 — Run the program\\n\\nMake sure your terminal is inside:\\n\\n```text\\nC:\\\\PROJECTS\\\\CN\\\\Real-Time-Network-Monitoring\\n```\\n\\nand your virtual environment is active:\\n\\n```text\\n(venv)\\n```\\n\\nThen run:\\n\\n```powershell\\npython src\\\\network_monitor.py\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 14 — Expected output\\n\\nYou should see something similar to:\\n\\n```text\\n======================================\\n     REAL-TIME NETWORK MONITOR\\n======================================\\n\\nNetwork Interface: Wi-Fi\\nInterface Speed: 100 Mbps\\n\\n--------------------------------------\\nTime: 19:30:01\\nUpload Speed: 2.43 Mbps\\nDownload Speed: 18.52 Mbps\\nPackets Sent: 125430\\nPackets Received: 182640\\nLatency: 21.0 ms\\nPacket Loss: 0.0 %\\nNetwork Utilization: 20.95 %\\n\\n--------------------------------------\\nTime: 19:30:02\\nUpload Speed: 3.12 Mbps\\nDownload Speed: 25.61 Mbps\\nPackets Sent: 125512\\nPackets Received: 182731\\nLatency: 23.0 ms\\nPacket Loss: 0.0 %\\nNetwork Utilization: 28.73 %\\n```\\n\\n**Your numbers will be different. That's expected.**\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 15 — How to stop it\\n\\nBecause:\\n\\n```python\\nwhile True:\\n```\\n\\nruns continuously, press:\\n\\n```text\\nCtrl + C\\n```\\n\\nto stop the program.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 📒 What to write in your project notes\\n\\nAdd this under **Phase 2**:\\n\\n```text\\nPHASE 2 — REAL-TIME NETWORK DATA COLLECTION\\n\\nFile:\\nsrc/network_monitor.py\\n\\nObjective:\\nTo continuously collect and calculate important network\\nperformance metrics.\\n\\nImplementation:\\n\\n1. Network Interface\\n   psutil is used to identify the active network interface.\\n\\n2. Network Traffic\\n   Bytes sent and bytes received are collected from the\\n   operating system.\\n\\n3. Upload Speed\\n   Calculated from the change in bytes sent over time.\\n\\n4. Download Speed\\n   Calculated from the change in bytes received over time.\\n\\n5. Packets Sent\\n   Obtained using psutil.\\n\\n6. Packets Received\\n   Obtained using psutil.\\n\\n7. Latency\\n   Measured using a ping request.\\n\\n8. Network Utilization\\n   Estimated using the current traffic rate and the\\n   network interface link speed.\\n\\nThe program repeats the collection process approximately\\nevery second.\\n\\nCurrent output:\\nThe metrics are displayed continuously in the terminal.\\n\\nStatus:\\nPhase 2 - Data collection implemented.\\n\\nNext:\\nStore each measurement with a timestamp so that the data\\ncan be analyzed and visualized later.\\n```\\n\\n## Your project structure now\\n\\n```text\\nReal-Time-Network-Monitoring/\\n│\\n├── data/                    ← future collected data\\n│\\n├── src/\\n│   └── network_monitor.py   ← THIS CODE\\n│\\n├── dashboard/               ← future dashboard\\n├── database/                ← future database\\n├── tests/                   ← future testing\\n├── screenshots/             ← save output screenshots here\\n├── documentation/           ← project notes\\n│\\n└── venv/                    ← Python environment\\n```\\n\\n### ⚠️ One thing before we continue\\n\\n**Don't create the dashboard yet. Now we will implement **Phase 2 completely**, but in a way that directly prepares the data needed for the final dashboard.\\n\\n# Phase 2 — Real-Time Network Data Collection\\n\\n### Goal\\n\\nCollect these 8 metrics continuously:\\n\\n1. Network traffic\\n2. Upload speed\\n3. Download speed\\n4. Packets sent\\n5. Packets received\\n6. Packet loss\\n7. Latency\\n8. Network utilization\\n\\n### Phase 2 flow\\n\\n```text\\nNetwork Interface\\n       ↓\\nCollect raw counters\\n       ↓\\nCalculate speed\\n       ↓\\nMeasure latency \u0026 packet loss\\n       ↓\\nCalculate utilization\\n       ↓\\nDisplay live metrics\\n       ↓\\nPhase 3: Store the data\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 1 — Create the collector file\\n\\nInside your project:\\n\\n```text\\nReal-Time-Network-Monitoring/\\n│\\n├── src/\\n│   └── network_monitor.py\\n```\\n\\nOpen `network_monitor.py`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 2 — Add the imports\\n\\n```python\\nimport psutil\\nimport time\\nimport platform\\nimport subprocess\\nimport re\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 3 — Select your network interface\\n\\nFirst, we need to know which interface you're using.\\n\\nAdd:\\n\\n```python\\ndef get_active_interface():\\n    interfaces = psutil.net_if_stats()\\n\\n    for name, stats in interfaces.items():\\n        if stats.isup and name.lower() not in [\\\"lo\\\", \\\"loopback\\\"]:\\n            return name\\n\\n    return None\\n```\\n\\nThis looks for an active interface such as:\\n\\n```text\\nWi-Fi\\nEthernet\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 4 — Collect raw network counters\\n\\nAdd:\\n\\n```python\\ndef get_network_stats():\\n    stats = psutil.net_io_counters()\\n\\n    return {\\n        \\\"bytes_sent\\\": stats.bytes_sent,\\n        \\\"bytes_received\\\": stats.bytes_recv,\\n        \\\"packets_sent\\\": stats.packets_sent,\\n        \\\"packets_received\\\": stats.packets_recv\\n    }\\n```\\n\\nThis gives us:\\n\\n```text\\nBytes Sent\\nBytes Received\\nPackets Sent\\nPackets Received\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 5 — Calculate upload/download speed\\n\\nWe need **two readings**.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nReading 1\\nBytes received = 100 MB\\n\\nReading 2\\nBytes received = 105 MB\\n```\\n\\nDifference:\\n\\n```text\\n105 - 100 = 5 MB\\n```\\n\\nIf the readings are one second apart:\\n\\n```text\\n5 MB/sec\\n```\\n\\nConvert that to Mbps.\\n\\nAdd:\\n\\n```python\\ndef calculate_speed(previous, current, elapsed):\\n    upload_bps = (current[\\\"bytes_sent\\\"] - previous[\\\"bytes_sent\\\"]) / elapsed\\n    download_bps = (current[\\\"bytes_received\\\"] - previous[\\\"bytes_received\\\"]) / elapsed\\n\\n    upload_mbps = (upload_bps * 8) / 1_000_000\\n    download_mbps = (download_bps * 8) / 1_000_000\\n\\n    return upload_mbps, download_mbps\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 6 — Measure latency and packet loss\\n\\nWe will use a simple ping test.\\n\\nAdd:\\n\\n```python\\ndef ping_host(host=\\\"8.8.8.8\\\"):\\n    system = platform.system()\\n\\n    if system == \\\"Windows\\\":\\n        command = [\\\"ping\\\", \\\"-n\\\", \\\"1\\\", \\\"-w\\\", \\\"1000\\\", host]\\n    else:\\n        command = [\\\"ping\\\", \\\"-c\\\", \\\"1\\\", \\\"-W\\\", \\\"1\\\", host]\\n\\n    result = subprocess.run(\\n        command,\\n        capture_output=True,\\n        text=True\\n    )\\n\\n    if result.returncode != 0:\\n        return None, 100.0\\n\\n    match = re.search(r\\\"time[=\u003c]\\\\s*(\\\\d+(?:\\\\.\\\\d+)?)\\\\s*ms\\\", result.stdout)\\n\\n    if match:\\n        latency = float(match.group(1))\\n        return latency, 0.0\\n\\n    return None, 100.0\\n```\\n\\nHere:\\n\\n```text\\n8.8.8.8\\n```\\n\\nis Google's public DNS server.\\n\\nWe are using it as a **measurement target**, not as part of your application.\\n\\nThe function gives:\\n\\n```text\\nLatency → milliseconds\\nPacket loss → percentage\\n```\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nLatency: 22 ms\\nPacket loss: 0%\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 7 — Network utilization\\n\\nNetwork utilization requires the network interface's link speed.\\n\\nAdd:\\n\\n```python\\ndef get_interface_speed(interface):\\n    stats = psutil.net_if_stats()\\n\\n    if interface in stats:\\n        return stats[interface].speed\\n\\n    return 0\\n```\\n\\nThen:\\n\\n```python\\ndef calculate_utilization(upload_mbps, download_mbps, interface_speed):\\n    if interface_speed \u003c= 0:\\n        return None\\n\\n    total_mbps = upload_mbps + download_mbps\\n    utilization = (total_mbps / interface_speed) * 100\\n\\n    return min(utilization, 100)\\n```\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nUpload = 8 Mbps\\nDownload = 42 Mbps\\nInterface = 100 Mbps\\n\\nUtilization =\\n(8 + 42) / 100 × 100\\n\\n= 50%\\n```\\n\\n**Note:** this is an estimate based on the interface's reported link speed, not your ISP's advertised Internet speed.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 8 — Create the main monitoring loop\\n\\nNow add:\\n\\n```python\\ndef main():\\n\\n    interface = get_active_interface()\\n\\n    print(\\\"======================================\\\")\\n    print(\\\" REAL-TIME NETWORK MONITOR\\\")\\n    print(\\\"======================================\\\")\\n\\n    print(\\\"Interface:\\\", interface)\\n\\n    if interface:\\n        interface_speed = get_interface_speed(interface)\\n        print(\\\"Interface Speed:\\\", interface_speed, \\\"Mbps\\\")\\n    else:\\n        interface_speed = 0\\n        print(\\\"No active interface found.\\\")\\n\\n    previous = get_network_stats()\\n    previous_time = time.time()\\n\\n    while True:\\n\\n        time.sleep(1)\\n\\n        current = get_network_stats()\\n        current_time = time.time()\\n\\n        elapsed = current_time - previous_time\\n\\n        upload, download = calculate_speed(\\n            previous,\\n            current,\\n            elapsed\\n        )\\n\\n        latency, packet_loss = ping_host()\\n\\n        utilization = calculate_utilization(\\n            upload,\\n            download,\\n            interface_speed\\n        )\\n\\n        print(\\\"\\\\n--------------------------------------\\\")\\n        print(\\\"Time:\\\", time.strftime(\\\"%H:%M:%S\\\"))\\n        print(\\\"Upload Speed:\\\", round(upload, 2), \\\"Mbps\\\")\\n        print(\\\"Download Speed:\\\", round(download, 2), \\\"Mbps\\\")\\n        print(\\\"Packets Sent:\\\", current[\\\"packets_sent\\\"])\\n        print(\\\"Packets Received:\\\", current[\\\"packets_received\\\"])\\n\\n        if latency is not None:\\n            print(\\\"Latency:\\\", round(latency, 2), \\\"ms\\\")\\n        else:\\n            print(\\\"Latency: Request timed out\\\")\\n\\n        print(\\\"Packet Loss:\\\", round(packet_loss, 2), \\\"%\\\")\\n\\n        if utilization is not None:\\n            print(\\\"Network Utilization:\\\", round(utilization, 2), \\\"%\\\")\\n        else:\\n            print(\\\"Network Utilization: Not available\\\")\\n\\n        previous = current\\n        previous_time = current_time\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    main()\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Step 9 — Run the project\\n\\nFrom your project root:\\n\\n```bash\\npython src/network_monitor.py\\n```\\n\\nYou should get something similar Project Title\\n\\n**Real-Time Network Monitoring, Performance \u0026 Traffic Analytics Platform**\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. Project Idea\\n\\nThe main idea of this project is to develop a platform that continuously monitors a computer network, collects network performance and traffic information, analyzes the collected data, and displays the results through an interactive dashboard.\\n\\nThe system will monitor parameters such as network traffic, upload speed, download speed, latency, packets sent and received, packet loss, and network utilization.\\n\\nThe collected data will be processed and analyzed to understand network performance and identify abnormal network behavior.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 3. Problem Statement\\n\\nIn a computer network, network performance can change continuously because of high traffic, network congestion, packet loss, high latency, or other problems.\\n\\nTraditional network monitoring tools may provide raw information, but understanding the collected data and identifying performance problems can be difficult.\\n\\nTherefore, this project aims to develop a system that continuously collects network-performance data, analyzes the data, identifies unusual network behavior, and presents useful information through a real-time dashboard.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 4. Main Objective\\n\\nThe main objective of this project is:\\n\\n**To develop a real-time network monitoring and analytics platform that collects, analyzes, and visualizes network performance and traffic data.**\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 5. Specific Objectives\\n\\nThe project will:\\n\\n1. Collect network performance data continuously.\\n2. Monitor upload and download traffic.\\n3. Monitor packets sent and received.\\n4. Measure network latency.\\n5. Monitor packet loss and network errors where available.\\n6. Store collected network data for historical analysis.\\n7. Analyze network performance using statistical methods.\\n8. Identify unusual network behavior using threshold-based anomaly detection.\\n9. Display network information using an interactive dashboard.\\n10. Generate alerts when important network parameters cross defined thresholds.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 6. Basic Working Principle\\n\\nThe project follows this process:\\n\\nNetwork\\n↓\\nData Collection\\n↓\\nData Processing\\n↓\\nData Storage\\n↓\\nData Analysis\\n↓\\nVisualization\\n↓\\nAlerts and Insights\\n\\nThe Python program will collect network information from the computer.\\n\\nThe collected data will then be cleaned and processed.\\n\\nThe processed data will be stored and analyzed.\\n\\nFinally, the results will be displayed on a dashboard.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 7. For **“Real-Time Network Monitoring, Performance \u0026 Traffic Analytics Platform”**, I’d recommend a **20-day execution plan** where you build a working version first and then add advanced features.\\n\\nThe goal is that by **Day 20**, you have a working demo, dashboard, documentation, PPT, and project explanation.\\n\\n# 🚀 20-Day Complete Project Plan\\n\\n## 🟢 PHASE 1 — Understand \u0026 Set Up\\n### Day 1 — Understand the project\\n\\nLearn only the CN concepts you actually need:\\n\\n- Network interface\\n- IP address\\n- Packets\\n- Bytes\\n- Bandwidth\\n- Upload/download\\n- Latency\\n- Packet loss\\n- TCP/UDP\\n- Ports\\n- Network utilization\\n\\nUnderstand this flow:\\n\\n```text\\nComputer / Network\\n        ↓\\nNetwork Metrics\\n        ↓\\nPython Collector\\n        ↓\\nData Processing\\n        ↓\\nDatabase\\n        ↓\\nAnalytics\\n        ↓\\nDashboard\\n        ↓\\nAlerts\\n```\\n\\n### Output of Day 1\\n\\nYou should be able to explain:\\n\\n\u003e \\\"What is my project doing?\\\"\\n\\nin 2–3 minutes.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 🟢 Day 2 — Set up development environment\\n\\nInstall:\\n\\n- Python\\n- VS Code\\n- Git\\n- Streamlit\\n- Pandas\\n- NumPy\\n- Plotly\\n- psutil\\n\\nCreate:\\n\\n```text\\nnetwork-monitoring/\\n│\\n├── data/\\n├── src/\\n├── dashboard/\\n├── database/\\n├── tests/\\n├── screenshots/\\n└── README.md\\n```\\n\\nCreate your first Python program and verify that everything works.\\n\\n### Output\\n\\nWorking Python environment.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 🟢 Day 3 — Explore your network\\n\\nWrite a small Python program to identify:\\n\\n- Hostname\\n- IP address\\n- Network interfaces\\n- Interface status\\n- MAC address where available\\n\\nExample output:\\n\\n```text\\nComputer: LAPTOP\\nIP: 192.168.1.10\\n\\nInterface:\\nWi-Fi\\n\\nStatus:\\nConnected\\n```\\n\\n### Output\\n\\n**Network information collector**\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 🟢 PHASE 2 — Build the Data Collector\\n\\n## Day 4 — Collect network traffic statistics\\n\\nUse `psutil` to collect:\\n\\n- Bytes sent\\n- Bytes received\\n- Packets sent\\n- Packets received\\n- Errors\\n- Dropped packets\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nTime       Sent      Received\\n10:01      2.1 MB    15.4 MB\\n10:02      2.4 MB    18.2 MB\\n10:03      3.0 MB    22.1 MB\\n```\\n\\n### Output\\n\\nYour program continuously collects network statistics.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Day 5 — Calculate network speed\\n\\nRaw bytes aren't very useful by themselves.\\n\\nCalculate:\\n\\n```text\\nDownload speed\\nUpload speed\\n```\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nDownload = 42.5 Mbps\\nUpload   = 8.3 Mbps\\n```\\n\\nStore:\\n\\n```text\\ntimestamp\\ndownload_speed\\nupload_speed\\n```\\n\\n### Output\\n\\nReal-time upload/download measurement.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Day 6 — Calculate latency\\n\\nImplement latency measurement.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nGoogle/server\\n       ↓\\nPing\\n       ↓\\nResponse time\\n```\\n\\nCollect:\\n\\n```text\\nTimestamp\\nLatency\\nPacket loss\\n```\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\n10:01 → 21 ms\\n10:02 → 19 ms\\n10:03 → 24 ms\\n```\\n\\n### Output\\n\\nWorking latency monitor.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Day 7 — Combine everything\\n\\nNow combine:\\n\\n```text\\nTimestamp\\nInterface\\nUpload\\nDownload\\nLatency\\nPackets sent\\nPackets received\\nErrors\\nDrops\\n```\\n\\ninto one dataset.\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n| Time | Download | Upload | Latency | Packet Loss |\\n|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|\\n| 10:01 | 42 | 8 | 21 | 0.1% |\\n| 10:02 | 45 | 9 | 19 | 0.0% |\\n| 10:03 | 38 | 7 | 24 | 0.2% |\\n\\n### Output\\n\\n🎯 **Your basic monitoring engine is complete.**\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 🟡 PHASE 3 — Data Storage \u0026 Processing\\n\\n## Day 8 — Save data\\n\\nInitially use CSV.\\n\\n```text\\ndata/\\n   network_data.csv\\n```\\n\\nEvery measurement should be appended.\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\ntimestamp,download,upload,latency,packet_loss\\n10:01,42,8,21,0.1\\n10:02,45,9,19,0.0\\n```\\n\\n### Output\\n\\nPersistent historical data.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Day 9 — Data cleaning\\n\\nUse Pandas.\\n\\nHandle:\\n\\n- Missing values\\n- Duplicate records\\n- Invalid values\\n- Timestamp formatting\\n- Outliers\\n\\nCreate a clean dataset.\\n\\n```text\\nRaw Data\\n   ↓\\nCleaning\\n   ↓\\nProcessed Data\\n```\\n\\n### Output\\n\\nClean analytics-ready dataset.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Day 10 — Build basic analytics\\n\\nCalculate:\\n\\n### Network statistics\\n\\n- Average download\\n- Maximum download\\n- Average upload\\n- Maximum upload\\n- Average latency\\n- Maximum latency\\n- Average packet loss\\n- Total data transferred\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nAverage Download: 38.5 Mbps\\nPeak Download:    72.3 Mbps\\n\\nAverage Latency:  28 ms\\nMaximum Latency:  184 ms\\n\\nAverage Loss:     0.4%\\n```\\n\\n### Output\\n\\nYour **analytics engine**.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 🟡 PHASE 4 — Dashboard\\n\\n## Day 11 — Create Streamlit dashboard\\n\\nCreate:\\n\\n```text\\ndashboard.py\\n```\\n\\nBuild the basic layout:\\n\\n```text\\nNETWORK MONITORING\\n────────────────────────\\n\\nDownload\\n42.5 Mbps\\n\\nUpload\\n8.3 Mbps\\n\\nLatency\\n21 ms\\n\\nPacket Loss\\n0.2%\\n```\\n\\n### Output\\n\\nBasic dashboard.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Day 12 — Add real-time charts\\n\\nAdd:\\n\\n### Chart 1\\n\\nDownload speed vs time\\n\\n### Chart 2\\n\\nUpload speed vs time\\n\\n### Chart 3\\n\\nLatency vs time\\n\\n### Chart 4\\n\\nPacket loss vs time\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nLatency\\n200 │             ╱╲\\n150 │            ╱  ╲\\n100 │     ╱╲   ╱    ╲\\n 50 │────╯  ╲─╯\\n  0 └──────────────────\\n       Time →\\n```\\n\\n### Output\\n\\nInteractive analytics dashboard.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Day 13 — Add filters\\n\\nAllow the user to select:\\n\\n- Time period\\n- Network interface\\n- Metric\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nTime Range:\\n[Last 10 minutes ▼]\\n\\nMetric:\\n[Latency ▼]\\n```\\n\\n### Output\\n\\nInteractive dashboard.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Day 14 — Add network status\\n\\nCreate status rules.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nLatency \u003c 50 ms\\n      ↓\\n🟢 NORMAL\\n```\\n\\n```text\\nLatency 50–100 ms\\n      ↓\\n🟠 WARNING\\n```\\n\\n```text\\nLatency \u003e 100 ms\\n      ↓\\n🔴 CRITICAL\\n```\\n\\nDo similar rules for:\\n\\n- Packet loss\\n- Network errors\\n- Utilization\\n\\n### Output\\n\\nAutomatic network health indicator.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 🔴 PHASE 5 — Analytics Intelligence\\n\\n## Day 15 — Traffic analysis\\n\\nAnalyze:\\n\\n\u003e When is network traffic highest?\\n\\nCalculate:\\n\\n- Peak traffic\\n- Average traffic\\n- Minimum traffic\\n- Traffic trends\\n\\nCreate:\\n\\n```text\\nHourly Traffic\\n       ↓\\nPeak period\\n       ↓\\nLow-usage period\\n```\\n\\n### Output\\n\\nTraffic behavior analysis.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Day 16 — Anomaly detection\\n\\nStart with a **simple rule-based system**.\\n\\nExample:\\n\\n```text\\nNormal latency = 10–50 ms\\n\\nCurrent = 180 ms\\n\\n             ↓\\n\\n       ANOMALY DETECTED\\n```\\n\\nDetect:\\n\\n- High latency\\n- High packet loss\\n- Sudden traffic spike\\n- Network downtime\\n\\nDon't start with machine learning.\\n\\nFirst make the basic system work.\\n\\n### Output\\n\\nAutomatic anomaly detection.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Day 17 — Alert system\\n\\nCreate an alert panel:\\n\\n```text\\nRECENT ALERTS\\n\\n🔴 10:42\\nHigh latency Create dashboard | 4–5 days |\\n| 5 | Add alerts + anomaly detection | 2–3 days |\\n| 6 | Testing + documentation + presentation | 2–3 days |\\n| **Total** | | **15–20 days** |\\n\\nIf you work only **2–3 hours/day**, expect around **3–4 weeks**.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 1. What is the project?\\n\\nThe basic idea is:\\n\\n\u003e **Continuously monitor network traffic, collect network-performance data, analyze it, and display the results on a real-time dashboard.**\\n\\nInstead of manually checking network performance, your system continuously observes the network.\\n\\n### Simple architecture\\n\\n```text\\n             NETWORK\\n                ↓\\n       ┌─────────────────┐\\n       │ Data Collection │\\n       │     Python      │\\n       └────────┬────────┘\\n                ↓\\n       ┌─────────────────┐\\n       │ Data Processing │\\n       │ \u0026 Analytics     │\\n       └────────┬────────┘\\n                ↓\\n       ┌─────────────────┐\\n       │ Database / CSV  │\\n       └────────┬────────┘\\n                ↓\\n       ┌─────────────────┐\\n       │   Dashboard     │\\n       │ Streamlit/Power │\\n       │ BI             │\\n       └────────┬────────┘\\n                ↓\\n        REAL-TIME INSIGHTS\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 2. What data will you collect?\\n\\nYour Python program can continuously collect information such as:\\n\\n### Network performance\\n\\n- Upload speed\\n- Download speed\\n- Latency\\n- Packet loss\\n- Network availability\\n- Connection status\\n\\n### Traffic information\\n\\n- Packets sent\\n- Packets received\\n- Bytes sent\\n- Bytes received\\n- Number of connections\\n- Protocol\\n- Source/destination information where appropriate\\n\\n### System information\\n\\nYou can also collect:\\n\\n- CPU usage\\n- RAM usage\\n- Network interface utilization\\n- Disk usage\\n\\nThis makes the project more interesting because you can investigate relationships such as:\\n\\n\u003e **Does high CPU usage affect network performance?**\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 3. What happens in real time?\\n\\nSuppose your computer is currently using the network.\\n\\nYour application might collect:\\n\\n```text\\nTime       Download   Upload   Latency   Packets\\n10:00:01   42 Mbps    8 Mbps    21 ms     1240\\n10:00:02   45 Mbps    9 Mbps    19 ms     1312\\n10:00:03   41 Mbps    8 Mbps    23 ms     1198\\n10:00:04   12 Mbps    2 Mbps    87 ms      640\\n```\\n\\nThe system identifies that something changed.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nNormal latency:     15–30 ms\\nCurrent latency:    87 ms\\n                   ↓\\n             HIGH LATENCY\\n                   ↓\\n              ALERT\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 4. Dashboard — the main output\\n\\nThis is where your project becomes visually impressive.\\n\\nYour dashboard can look conceptually like:\\n\\n```text\\n╔══════════════════════════════════════════════╗\\n║       NETWORK MONITORING DASHBOARD           ║\\n╠══════════════════════════════════════════════╣\\n║                                              ║\\n║  Download     Upload      Latency   Packet   ║\\n║  42.5 Mbps    8.2 Mbps    21 ms    0.3%     ║\\n║                                              ║\\n╠══════════════════════════════════════════════╣\\n║                                              ║\\n║       Network Traffic - Live                 ║\\n║       📈                                      ║\\n║       ╱╲     ╱╲                              ║\\n║   ╱╲╱  ╲╱╲╱  ╲                              ║\\n║                                              ║\\n╠══════════════════════════════════════════════╣\\n║                                              ║\\n║ Latency Trend            Packet Loss         ║\\n║ 📈                       📊                   ║\\n║                                              ║\\n╠══════════════════════════════════════════════╣\\n║ Network Status: 🟢 NORMAL                    ║\\n║                                              ║\\n║ Alerts:                                      ║\\n║ ⚠ High latency detected at 10:04            ║\\n╚══════════════════════════════════════════════╝\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 5. Important KPIs\\n\\nYour dashboard should have **5–8 main KPIs**.\\n\\n### Recommended:\\n\\n**1. Current Download Speed**\\n\\n```text\\n42.5 Mbps\\n```\\n\\n**2. Current Upload Speed**\\n\\n```text\\n8.2 Mbps\\n```\\n\\n**3. Average Latency**\\n\\n```text\\n21 ms\\n```\\n\\n**4. Packet Loss**\\n\\n```text\\n0.3%\\n```\\n\\n**5. Network Utilization**\\n\\n```text\\n68%\\n```\\n\\n**6. Active Connections**\\n\\n```text\\n27\\n```\\n\\n**7. Total Data Transferred**\\n\\n```text\\n2.4 GB\\n```\\n\\n**8. Network Status**\\n\\n```text\\n🟢 Normal\\n🟠 Warning\\n🔴 Critical\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 6. Data analytics part\\n\\nThis is important because you don't want your project to become **just a network monitoring tool**.\\n\\nYou are doing **analytics** on the collected network data.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n### Trend analysis\\n\\nYou can answer:\\n\\n\u003e When is network traffic highest?\\n\\nYour graph might show:\\n\\n```text\\nTraffic\\n  │\\n  │              ╱╲\\n  │          ╱╲ ╱  ╲\\n  │      ╱╲ ╱  ╲    ╲\\n  │____╱__╲___________╲____\\n       10  11  12  1   2\\n                 Time\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Performance analysis\\n\\nYou can calculate:\\n\\n```text\\nAverage latency\\nMaximum latency\\nMinimum latency\\nAverage packet loss\\nPeak bandwidth\\nAverage bandwidth\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 7. Add anomaly detection\\n\\nThis is one of the features that can make your project much better.\\n\\nThe system learns what is **normal** based on your collected data.\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```text\\nNormal latency:\\n10–40 ms\\n\\nCurrent:\\n180 ms\\n\\n       ↓\\n\\nANOMALY DETECTED\\n```\\n\\nYou don't need complicated AI.\\n\\nInitially you can use simple statistical rules:\\n\\n```text\\nif latency \u003e threshold:\\n       generate alert\\n```\\n\\nLater you can upgrade it using:\\n\\n- Moving average\\n- Z-score\\n- Isolation Forest\\n- Time-series anomaly detection\\n\\nSo your project can evolve.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 8. Alert system\\n\\nYour system can generate alerts such as:\\n\\n```text\\n⚠ WARNING\\n\\nHigh latency detected\\n\\nCurrent latency: 156 ms\\nNormal range: 15–40 ms\\nTime: 10:42 PM\\n```\\n\\nAnother example:\\n\\n```text\\n🔴 CRITICAL\\n\\nPacket loss detected\\n\\nPacket loss: 12.4%\\n```\\n\\nYou can later add:\\n\\n- Desktop notifications\\n- Email alerts\\n- Telegram alerts\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 9. Technology stack\\n\\nFor **your project**, I would keep the first version simple.\\n\\n### Backend/data collection\\n\\n**Python**\\n\\nUseful libraries:\\n\\n```text\\npsutil\\nsocket\\nsubprocess\\npandas\\ntime\\n```\\n\\nFor more advanced packet/network analysis, you can later explore:\\n\\n```text\\nScapy\\nPyShark\\n```\\n\\n### Dashboard\\n\\nI recommend:\\n\\n**Streamlit**\\n\\nbecause you can build the dashboard much faster than creating a complete frontend.\\n\\n### Analytics\\n\\n```text\\nPandas\\nNumPy\\nMatplotlib\\nPlotly\\n```\\n\\n### Storage\\n\\nStart with:\\n\\n```text\\nCSV\\n```\\n\\nThen upgrade to:\\n\\n```text\\nSQLite\\n```\\n\\nif you want a more realistic system.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 10. Your final project output\\n\\nAt the end, you should be able to demonstrate something like this:\\n\\n### Step 1 — Start application\\n\\n```text\\npython monitor.py\\n```\\n\\n↓\\n\\n### Step 2 — Network data starts collecting\\n\\n```text\\nTimestamp       Latency    Download\\n10:00:01        22 ms      41 Mbps\\n10:00:02        19 ms      44 Mbps\\n10:00:03        25 ms      39 Mbps\\n```\\n\\n↓\\n\\n### Step 3 — Dashboard updates\\n\\n```text\\nDOWNLOAD\\n42.3 Mbps\\n\\nUPLOAD\\n8.1 Mbps\\n\\nLATENCY\\n23 ms\\n\\nPACKET LOSS\\n0.2%\\n\\nSTATUS\\n🟢 NORMAL\\n```\\n\\n↓\\n\\n### Step 4 — Traffic increases\\n\\n```text\\nLatency = 175 ms\\n```\\n\\n↓\\n\\n### Step 5 — Analytics engine detects abnormal behavior\\n\\n```text\\n⚠ ANOMALY DETECTED\\n```\\n\\n↓\\n\\n### Step 6 — Dashboard displays the event\\n\\n```text\\nRecent Alerts\\n\\n10:42 PM\\nHigh latency\\n\\n10:44 PM\\nPacket loss detected\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 11. What makes it a \\\"Data Analytics\\\" project?\\n\\nThis distinction is important for your faculty.\\n\\nDon't say:\\n\\n\u003e \\\"My project monitors the network.\\\"\\n\\nSay:\\n\\n\u003e **\\\"The project continuously collects network-performance and traffic metrics and applies statistical analysis, trend analysis and anomaly detection to identify performance degradation and provide real-time visual insights.\\\"**\\n\\nThat clearly connects **Computer Networks + Data Analytics**.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# 12. Future improvements\\n\\nYou can start simple and keep adding features.\\n\\n### Version 1 — Basic\\n\\n```text\\nNetwork data\\n     ↓\\nPython\\n     ↓\\nCSV\\n     ↓\\nStreamlit dashboard\\n```\\n\\n### Version 2 — Analytics\\n\\nAdd:\\n\\n```text\\nTrend analysis\\nPeak traffic\\nAverage performance\\nAnomaly detection\\n```\\n\\n### Version 3 — Advanced\\n\\nAdd:\\n\\n```text\\nSQLite\\nMultiple devices\\nProtocol analysis\\nAlerts\\nHistorical analysis\\n```\\n\\n### Version 4 — More realistic\\n\\nAdd:\\n\\n```text\\nNetwork topology\\nDevice-wise monitoring\\nPredictive traffic analysis\\nAutomatic recommendations\\nCloud deployment\\n```\\n\\nSo you don't have to build the entire advanced system initially.\\n\\n---\\n\\n# ⏱️ Realistic timeline for YOU\\n\\nI would recommend **20 days**, rather than trying to finish everything in 7–10 days.\\n\\n### Days 1–3\\nUnderstand CN concepts + project architecture\\n\\n### Days 4–6\\nBuild network data collector\\n\\n### Days 7–9\\nStore and clean the data\\n\\n### Days 10–13\\nBuild Streamlit dashboard\\n\\n### Days 14–16\\nAdd analytics + graphs\\n\\n### Days 17–18\\nAdd anomaly detection + alerts\\n\\n### Days 19–20\\nTesting + documentation + PPT + demo\\n\\n**Final result:** a working **real-time CN + Data Analytics platform** that you can demonstrate live on your laptop.\\n\\nIf your goal is a project that is **simple enough to build yourself but expandable into a strong final-year project**, this architecture is particularly suitable because you can start with one computer/network interface and later expand to multiple devices, historical analytics, alerts, and predictive
